113 //*************************************************************
114 /*
115  The I-value of an element depends on whether the element
116  is part of a compound or not. poth[] is the array
117  containing I-values of elements when the elements are
118  present in their elemental form. potgas[] and potcon[]
119  and are I-values of elements when they are part of
120  a compound.
121  Further details are given below
122 */
123 
124 // poth is an array of length 100 and is again indexed by atomic number
125 // the table contais the I values of elements in the
126 // condensed phase (ICRU 37 - table 4.3). For example (poth[99] is the I-value of Fermium)
127 // * I-val of graphite in (ICRU 37 - table 4.3) is 78 eV. However in ESTAR 81 eV is used (poth[5]), as in ICRU 90.
128 // * I-val of chlorine in (ICRU 37 - table 4.3) is 174 eV. However in ESTAR 159.29 eV is used (poth[16]) (perhaps this was an improvement).
129 const double poth[] = {19.2,41.8,40.0,63.7,76.0,81.0,82.0,95.0,
130  115.,137.,149.,156.,166.,173.,173.,180.,159.29,
131  188.,190.,191.,216.,233.,245.,257.,272.,286.,
132  297.,311.,322.,330.,334.,350.,347.,348.,357.,
133  352.,363.,366.,379.,393.,417.,424.,428.,441.,
134  449.,470.,470.,469.,488.,488.,487.,485.,491.,
135  482.,488.,491.,501.,523.,535.,546.,560.,574.,
136  580.,591.,614.,628.,650.,658.,674.,684.,694.,
137  705.,718.,727.,736.,746.,757.,790.,790.,800.,
138  810.,823.,823.,830.,825.,794.,827.,826.,841.,
139  847.,878.,890.,902.,921.,934.,939.,952.,966.,
140  980.,994.
141  };
142 
143 // potgas represents the I-value of elements (atomic number 1-9)
144 // when the elements (atomic number 1-9) are part of a compound in a gaseous state (ICRU 37 - table 5.1)
145 const double potgas[] = {19.2,41.8,34.0,38.6,49.0,70.0,82.0, 97.0, 115.0};
146 // potcon represents the I-value of elements (atomic number 1-9)
147 // when the elements (atomic number 1-9) are part of a compound in a solid/liquid state (ICRU 37 - table 5.1)
148 const double potcon[] = {19.2,41.8,45.2,72.0,85.9,81.0,82.0, 106.0, 112.0};
149 
150 // elementData[] encodes ESTAR density-effect correction data for elements with atomic numbers Z from 1 to 100.
151 // The array is a flat sequence of per-element blocks with the following layout:
152 //
153 // <nshells>, 113 — number of subshells; energy grid size (always 113)
154 // <Z> — atomic number
155 // <I>, [oscillator...] — mean excitation energy in eV; for heavier elements
156 // (roughly Z > 10), additional oscillator-strength
157 // parameters follow
158 // [nj...], <-sentinel> — subshell occupation counts, terminated by a negative
159 // value sentinel (heavier elements only)
160 // [Ej...] — subshell binding energies in eV (heavier elements only)
161 // <delta[0..112]> — 113 density-effect correction values on the fixed
162 // energy grid defined in energy_grid[]
163 //
164 // See estar_dataParser.cpp for the exact parsing logic, and ICRU Report 37 for the
165 // underlying physics.
166 const double elementData[14532] = {
167  1,113
